response

I'll read the source and supporting files first.

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan, the block contract, and the correctness rules.

Plan adherence. The edit touches only module bilinearintrp (lines 2794–2908); the harness subtree check (sim_run.log:3) confirms the rest of the file is unchanged, and the grep for ul/vl/wl/i1r…/rul… finds no surviving reference in this module (the hits at raygentop.v:2706–2756 are linearmap's own regs, as the plan noted). All five edit steps are present: the old reg declarations and the old always @(posedge clk) are gone, the six difference wires, three 37-bit result wires and six 7-bit delay regs are added, three int_sop_2 are instantiated, and the three output assigns are replaced.

Block contract. Each of sop_r/sop_g/sop_b connects every input port by name — clk, reset(1'b0), mode_sigs(12'b0), ax, ay, bx, by, chainin(37'b0) — with widths matching: {10'b0,u}=18, {12'b0,d?u}=19, {10'b0,v}=18, {12'b0,d?v}=19, chainin=37, result→37-bit wire. chainout is left unconnected, so nothing reads it. Both product ports are used; chainin is tied to the constant 0 as the un-chained tie-off requires. Three blocks = 3 DSP tiles, within the 132 available.

Terms and arithmetic. Original (raygentop.v, pre-edit lines 2862–2886): r = (ul*rul + vl*rvl + wl*rwl) truncated to 7 bits, with wl = (255-u-v) mod 256. Since 256 ≡ 0 and 255 ≡ −1 (mod 128), u·ru + v·rv + wl·rw ≡ u·(ru−rw) + v·(rv−rw) − rw (mod 128); the 7-bit wrap of dru,drv and the block's zero-extension/2³⁷ wrap all change the value by multiples of 128, so res_r[6:0] is exact. Inside the block nothing wraps anyway (each product ≤ 255·127 = 32385, sum ≤ 64770 < 2³⁷). The discarded wl·rw term reappears as the −rw2 subtract outside the block, which the rules allow for a term with no matching port. Spot check u=200,v=100,ru=10,rv=20,rw=30: original 10330 mod 128 = 90; rewrite (33400 mod 128) − 30 = 120 − 30 = 90.

Registers and alignment. Original: stage 1 latches ul,vl,wl,r?l… at edge n, stage 2 latches the products at edge n+1. Rewrite: the block's operand registers latch u,v,dru,drv (all combinational from the same inputs) at edge n and result loads at edge n+1, while rw1/rw2 carry rw from the same sample through the same two edges. Both versions present the value of inputs sampled before edge n on r,g,b during (n+1, n+2); the impulse probe (sim_run.log:24–27) shows the matching 2-cycle latency. reset tied low is correct: bilinearintrp has no reset port (line 2762) and the original registers have no reset, so there is no reset behaviour to reproduce.

Visibility and power-up. Everything moved inside the blocks (ul,vl,wl, the weight regs, the nine products) was local to bilinearintrp and read only by the deleted lines; the module's only outputs r,g,b are still computed in soft logic. The added registers are X for the first two edges, which the power-up clause permits, and no logic relies on their initial value. No ASSUMES: line is needed — the rewrite is unconditional on input values.

2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, raygentop.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: int_sop_2 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37]
  • output ports: result[37], chainout[37]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 37 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 37 bits (modulo 2^37), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx, by) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above; a product-term port the rewrite does not use is tied to 0. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019

out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

In every row, operation: sop.
In every row, instances: 1.
In every row, register stages between the products and their sum: products registered 1 stage(s) before the sum.
In every row, products in the whole sum this one belongs to: 3 products in one chain of adders.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Objective

Minimize area × delay against the original design. Among rewrites that lower it, choose the one that lowers it most.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or none expected to lower area × delay, or the best result so far is the best available. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).

What is currently built and measured

The kept candidate's plan

TIER: T2
REGIONS: bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2874$348, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2875$349, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2876$350, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2877$351, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2878$352, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2879$353, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2880$354, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2881$355, bilinearintrp.$mul$raygentop.v:2882$356
SHARED_REDUCTION: the three sums in bilinearintrp: r (i1r+i2r+i3r), g (i1g+i2g+i3g), b (i1b+i2b+i3b) — one block per sum
BLOCKS: 3
MAPPING:

  • u, v stay exactly as they are: the combinational reg [7:0] written by the always @(selectuv or ...) case at lines 2818-2858.
  • New combinational 7-bit difference wires in bilinearintrp (7-bit arithmetic, wrapping mod 128):
    wire [6:0] dru = ru - rw; wire [6:0] drv = rv - rw;
    wire [6:0] dgu = gu - gw; wire [6:0] dgv = gv - gw;
    wire [6:0] dbu = bu - bw; wire [6:0] dbv = bv - bw;
  • Block R: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,dru}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,drv}, chainin=37'b0, result=res_r (37-bit wire), chainout unconnected.
  • Block G: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,dgu}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,dgv}, chainin=37'b0, result=res_g, chainout unconnected.
  • Block B: ax={10'b0,u}, ay={12'b0,dbu}, bx={10'b0,v}, by={12'b0,dbv}, chainin=37'b0, result=res_b, chainout unconnected.
  • Every block: clk=clk, reset=1'b0, mode_sigs=12'b0.
  • New 7-bit soft registers carrying the leftover term through the same two stages the products pass: rw1,rw2,gw1,gw2,bw1,bw2, clocked by clk (rw1<=rw; rw2<=rw1; etc.).
  • Outputs: assign r = res_r[6:0] - rw2; assign g = res_g[6:0] - gw2; assign b = res_b[6:0] - bw2;

PLAN:

Why this revision. The measured first pass bought a 4.9% shorter period with 6 blocks, but 6 DSP tiles alone cost +3 tiles over the baseline (+761k of the +650k measured area change), so area×delay came out +8.06%. The timing report shows the critical path is not inside the block and not the old soft multiply: it runs from a flip-flop in the upstream intersection logic through the MIN/sub chain and the u mux into int_sop_2.ax[6]. That path exists with any number of blocks. So the whole cost of the first pass was the extra DSP tiles, and the whole win was removing the nine soft 8×7 multiplies — which can be done with three blocks instead of six, leaving the DSP count at the baseline's 6 tiles (3 for the nine matrix multiplies at lines 335-343, which the measurement showed pack into 3 tiles, plus our 3). That should keep D/D0 near 0.95 at A/A0 ≈ 1.00 or a little under.

The regrouping (this is what makes 3 blocks enough). The region is three independent 3-product sums, each truncated to 7 bits, and the third weight is derived from the first two: wl = (255-u-v) mod 256 (line 2862). Only 7 bits of each sum survive, so work mod 128:

r ≡ u·ru + v·rv + wl·rw ≡ u·ru + v·rv + (255-u-v)·rw ≡ u·(ru-rw) + v·(rv-rw) + 255·rw (mod 128)

The 256k slop of wl's wrap is a multiple of 128 and vanishes; 255·rw ≡ -rw (mod 128). So each colour becomes two products plus one linear term: r = (u·dru + v·drv - rw) mod 128 with dru=(ru-rw) mod 128, drv=(rv-rw) mod 128. Two products is exactly one int_sop_2. The differences may be taken mod 128 because u·A ≡ u·(A mod 128) (mod 128), and the block's zero-extension of the 7-bit dru to 19 bits, plus its mod-2^37 result wrap, are both changes by multiples of 128, so the low 7 bits of result are exact. Products are at most 255·127 = 32385 and each block's sum at most 64770, so nothing wraps inside the block anyway. The -rw term has no block port and is therefore computed outside, as the correctness rules allow.

Worked check (u=200, v=100, ru=10, rv=20, rw=30): original wl=211, sum = 2000+2000+6330 = 10330, low 7 bits = 90. Rewrite: dru=108, drv=118, result = 21600+11800 = 33400, low 7 bits = 120, minus 30 → 90. Same.

Every candidate region. All nine are converted; none is left in soft logic. The six u/v products (lines 2874, 2875, 2877, 2878, 2880, 2881) become the ax·ay and bx·by terms of blocks R, G, B. The three w products (lines 2876, 2879, 2882) are regrouped away by the substitution above: their -u·rw/-v·rw parts are folded into the block operands dru/drv, and their 255·rw ≡ -rw part becomes the 7-bit subtract on the block's output. No multiplier remains in bilinearintrp.

Blocks added: 3, one per colour sum, each standalone. Chaining is not usable here: chainout is combinational from a block's input registers, so a chained pair costs 3 cycles from operands to the downstream result, while the module must produce r,g,b 2 cycles after its inputs are sampled; there is no earlier tap to borrow a cycle from (u,v are combinational from the module ports). chainin is tied to 0 on all three and chainout is left unconnected, as nothing may legally read it.

Register stages, per converted region (all nine identical).

  • Before: 2 stages. Stage 1 = ul/vl/wl and the nine weight registers (lines 2862-2873); stage 2 = the product registers i1r..i3b (lines 2874-2882); then the 3-input sum is combinational into r/g/b.
  • After: 2 stages. Stage 1 = the block's operand registers (taking the role of ul/vl and the weight registers); stage 2 = the block's result register (taking the role of the product registers, with the two-product sum formed inside); then the 7-bit subtract is combinational into r/g/b. Operands applied before edge k reach result after edge k+1 in both versions.

Cycle-exact alignment. Let the module inputs be sampled just before edge n. Original: at edge n, ul,vl,wl,rul,rvl,rwl,... load; at edge n+1 the nine products load; so r during (n+1,n+2) is the function of the values sampled before edge n. Rewrite: each block samples u,v,dru,drv (all combinational from the same inputs) at edge n and loads result at edge n+1, so res_r during (n+1,n+2) is that same sample; rw1 loads rw at edge n and rw2 loads rw1 at edge n+1, so rw2 during (n+1,n+2) is rw sampled before edge n — the same sample the original's rwl·wl product used. res_r[6:0] - rw2 in 7-bit arithmetic therefore equals (i1r+i2r+i3r) mod 128 on every cycle, and likewise for g, b.

Visibility. ul,vl,wl,rul..bwl,i1r..i3b are declared at lines 2796-2816 and read only at lines 2874-2886 inside this module; the module's only outputs are r,g,b (lines 2786-2791), used at line 2012 as blr,blg,blb. Nothing outside reads a value that moves inside a block. The ul/vl seen near lines 2735-2756 belong to linearmap, a different module, and are untouched.

Reset and power-up. bilinearintrp has no reset port and the testbench drives none ("reset policy: none"), so every block's reset is tied to 1'b0; there is no design reset whose behaviour must be reproduced. The blocks' registers and the six new 7-bit soft registers are unknown for the first two clock edges; the testbench's 8-cycle all-zero lead-in ends well after that, and with zero inputs both versions settle to r=g=b=0 before the comparison starts. Nothing relies on an added register's initial value.

Timing of the new soft logic. The six difference subtracts sit on the ru..bw path, which comes straight from shadedata = tm3_sram_data_in (line 676), a top-level input pad, so that path is pad-to-register and is not part of the register-to-register period; it costs area only (six 7-bit subtracts). On the output side, the original's 3-input 7-bit add is replaced by a 7-bit subtract of equal depth. Net soft-logic change: remove 222 flip-flops (the ul/vl/wl, nine weights, nine 15-bit products), the 8-bit 255-u-v, and the three 3-input 15-bit adds; add 42 flip-flops, six 7-bit subtracts and three 7-bit subtracts. Area should fall slightly below the baseline while the period stays at the first pass's ~4.6 ns.

Edits for the editor, inside module bilinearintrp only (lines 2794-2886).

  1. Delete the reg declarations at lines 2796-2816 (ul,vl,wl,i1b..i3r,rul..bwl). Keep reg [7:0] u; reg [7:0] v; (2794-2795) and the combinational always at 2818-2858 unchanged.
  2. Delete the whole always @(posedge clk) block at lines 2860-2883.
  3. Add the six difference wires, the three 37-bit result wires res_r,res_g,res_b, the six 7-bit regs rw1,rw2,gw1,gw2,bw1,bw2 and the always @(posedge clk) block that shifts them, exactly as in MAPPING.
  4. Instantiate the three int_sop_2 blocks by name, connecting every input port (clk, reset, mode_sigs, ax, ay, bx, by, chainin) and leaving chainout unconnected.
  5. Replace lines 2884-2886 with the three 7-bit subtracts in MAPPING.

No other module and nothing outside bilinearintrp changes.

DESIGN_CHANGE: If bilinearintrp could take one extra cycle of latency on r,g,b (or if u/v arrived one register stage earlier from resultwriter), the critical path that now ends at the block's ax input register — the MIN/sub chain in the intersection logic feeding the u/v mux — would be cut by that stage, which is the delay term this rewrite cannot touch; the block count would stay 3.

The kept candidate's edit, as a diff against the unmodified source as given to you

Reference simulation

After the edit, the framework simulates the edited design against the original with the reference testbench, comparing every output of bilinearintrp on every cycle after the testbench's lead-in phase (its first 8 cycles). A candidate that does not match is not measured. The testbench (tb_equiv_bilin.v) and the block's behavioural model (int_sop_2.v) are in sim_reference/.

Read these files in this directory: raygentop.v

response

I'll read the source around the candidate region and the surrounding context before deciding.

TIER: NONE
REGIONS: NONE
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE
BLOCKS: 0
MAPPING: NONE
PLAN:

Decision. Keep revision 1 (3 blocks, A/A0 0.977, D/D0 0.997, product 0.974). I propose no further rewrite: the kept candidate already converts all nine candidate products, uses the provably minimum number of blocks for them, and its period is set by a path that the rewrite cannot touch. Every remaining idea I can justify from the source changes area by a few LUTs — far inside the ±4.6% seed noise — or makes the result worse.

Every candidate region, converted or left. All nine ($mul$raygentop.v:2874$348 … $mul$raygentop.v:2882$356) are already converted in the kept revision and none is left in soft logic: the six u/v products are the ax*ay/bx*by terms of blocks R, G, B, and the three w products are regrouped away by u·ru+v·rv+wl·rw ≡ u·(ru−rw)+v·(rv−rw)−rw (mod 128), lines 2862–2882 with wl = 255−u−v and the 7‑bit outputs at 2884–2886. There is no tenth multiply in bilinearintrp and nothing left to convert. Blocks added by this plan: 0 (the kept candidate's 3 stand).

Register stages, converted regions (unchanged from the kept revision, stated for completeness). All nine: before the rewrite 2 stages (stage 1 = ul/vl/wl and the nine weight registers, lines 2862–2873; stage 2 = the product registers i1r..i3b, lines 2874–2882; then a combinational 3‑input add into r/g/b). After: 2 stages (stage 1 = the block's operand registers, stage 2 = the block's result register, then the combinational 7‑bit subtract of rw2/gw2/bw2). Operands applied before edge k reach the output after edge k+1 in both.

Why 3 blocks cannot be reduced. After the mod‑128 regrouping the region is exactly six products, u·dcu and v·dcv for c ∈ {r,g,b}. One int_sop_2 provides two product slots, and chaining adds sums, not slots, so six products need three blocks unless two colours share one block by bit‑field packing. Packing is impossible here: putting colour g at bit offset K via ay = dru + (dgu<<K), by = drv + (dgv<<K) needs K+7 ≤ 19, i.e. K ≤ 12, while the low field u·dru + v·drv reaches 2·127·127 = 32258, so K ≥ 15 is required to keep its carry out of the high field. The operands cannot be shrunk further — u, v are already reducible only to 7 bits (mod 128) and the differences are 7 bits — and the carry floor(L/2^K) cannot be corrected from the block's output, since only L mod 128 is visible there. Three DSP tiles is therefore the floor, and it already equals what the baseline spent on these same nine multiplies (both rows measure 6 tiles), so no block-count move is available in either direction that helps.

Why the period cannot be improved by a rewrite. The measured seed‑1 path starts at $dffe~387^Q~4, crosses $add~377 and the 16‑stage $add~380 carry chain, then three LUT levels (new_n6116_1, new_n6115, n4829) and ends at a block operand register. That is the intersection‑unit arithmetic feeding u01a..u10c/v01a..v10c and the selectuv case mux of lines 2818–2858 — all of it upstream of, or inside, logic the rewrite must reproduce unchanged, and over 3.7 ns of it is wire. The same path exists in the baseline, ending at ul/vl instead (baseline 4.847 ns vs 4.834 ns here), which is why D/D0 is 0.997 and why the first pass's 6 blocks bought nothing on delay either. The block itself has ~2.6 ns of slack (its internal path is 2.219 ns), but nothing on the critical path is multiply‑add, so none of it can be moved inside. Moving the mux behind the block's operand register would add a third stage and break the cycle‑exact match; there is no other stage in bilinearintrp to give back (module input → stage 1 → products → combinational output).

Why the remaining soft-logic trims are not worth a measurement. What the rewrite still adds is six 7‑bit difference subtracts on the pad path, 42 flip-flops delaying rw/gw/bw by the same two stages, and three 7‑bit output subtracts. The 42 flip-flops are irreducible: the leftover term is forced to be −rw (it is the value of the sum at u=v=0, for every choice of expansion base), it must arrive two cycles late, and it cannot ride through the block, because a block can only emit a delayed copy of an operand that is multiplied by a power of two in one of its two slots, and both slots are committed to u·dru and v·drv. Narrowing ax/bx to u[6:0]/v[6:0] is legal (changing u by multiples of 128 leaves result[6:0] fixed) but deletes only the bit‑7 mux — a couple of LUTs on 4.65e6, two orders of magnitude below the seed noise. Replacing one colour's block with hand‑coded mod‑128 multipliers to save a DSP tile (253 779) costs two 7×7 truncated multipliers plus ~42 pipeline flip-flops, which I estimate at 280k–390k, i.e. a net loss, and it puts a multiply on the already-critical mux path.

The kept revision is therefore the best result available from this block on this region, and it is below the original (median product 2.24585e7 vs 2.30616e7).

DESIGN_CHANGE: The only remaining lever is the path the rewrite may not touch: if bilinearintrp could take one more cycle of latency on r,g,b, or if u01a..u10c/v01a..v10c (or the muxed u/v) arrived one register stage later from the intersection logic, the MIN/sub carry chain plus selectuv mux that now ends at the block's operand register would be split across two stages, cutting the register-to-register period well below 4.8 ns at the same 3 blocks and the same area.

1	// int_sop_2: result = ax*ay + bx*by + chainin, unsigned, modulo 2^37 (the sum wraps at the
2	// result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[18], by[19], chainin[37] in; result[37],
4	// chainout[37] out. mode_sigs is not read.
5	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
6	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx, by and chainin alike); the products
7	// and their sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
8	// Cycles: every operand and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
9	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (operands and
10	// chainin to chainout 1).
11	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
12	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
13	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
14	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
15	// X until the first clear.
16	module int_sop_2 (
17	    input clk,
18	    input reset,
19	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
20	    input [17:0] ax, input [18:0] ay,
21	    input [17:0] bx, input [18:0] by,
22	    input [36:0] chainin,
23	    output reg [36:0] result,
24	    output [36:0] chainout
25	);
26	
27	    // stage 1: the input registers
28	    reg [17:0] ax_reg, bx_reg;
29	    reg [18:0] ay_reg, by_reg;
30	    reg [36:0] chainin_reg;
31	
32	    // combinational middle: the two products and their sum, from the registers
33	    wire [36:0] prod_a = ax_reg * ay_reg;                        // 18x19 -> 37, exact
34	    wire [36:0] prod_b = bx_reg * by_reg;
35	    wire [36:0] sum    = prod_a + prod_b + chainin_reg;          // modulo 2^37
36	
37	    always @(posedge clk) begin
38	        if (reset) begin
39	            result <= 0;
40	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; by_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
41	        end
42	        else begin
43	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; by_reg <= by; chainin_reg <= chainin;
44	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
45	        end
46	    end
47	
48	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
49	
50	endmodule

Objective loop

2 rewrite(s) measured · stopped by: planner declined
revisions allowed
9
seeds per rewrite
1, 2, 3, 4, 5 (adaptive)
kept
Pass 2
loop stopped by
planner declined (no representative mapping)
objective
area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02)
baseline
layout koios_extra_small · used area 4.7578e+06 · critical path 4.84712 ns · wirelength 25664 · area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02) 2.30616e+07 (read from entry; median per term over the entry's seed runs (its seed_medians; seeds lists them))

The objective is area x delay (delay register to register, 2026-11-02): A times D, from VPR's total used logic block area (I/Os and empty grid tiles excluded), A, the delay D (the register-to-register figure since 2026-10-20) and the total routed wirelength, W (all three recorded under every objective). Each term is the median over the loop's seeds; the objective is formed from the medians. Beside every product stand the ratios against the baseline, A/A0 (area), D/D0 (critical path), W/W0 (wirelength) and their product, so the normalization can be read off. After each measured rewrite, the planner is given every measurement so far and asked for a revision. A revision is built and checked like the first rewrite, and the lowest product is kept, a later rewrite having to beat it by more than 4.60%. The loop stops when the planner declines to revise, when a revision does not lower the best product by more than 4.60%, when a revision fails its checks, or when 9 revisions have been made.

Stated: the BLOCKS, TILES and STAGE_NS lines of the plan header. Measured: the packed DSP tile count from the gate flow, and the median period, area and wirelength over the seeds. "A/A0 · D/D0 · W/W0" are the rewrite's medians over the baseline's, whose product is the "vs baseline" change plus one. "vs previous" is against the rewrite measured before this one. "Path 1 ends in" is read from the rewrite's own timing report and netlist: the constant coefficients on the block (or plain multiply) that path 1 passes through, matched against the constants each module of the edited RTL multiplies by; two modules that share a coefficient set are both named, and the hover text gives the element and its constants. Verification comes from verification.yaml in the run dir when one was written; the loop itself does not run the equivalence checks. Its label is proven when the LEC closed, simulation under assumption when the simulation passed without a closed proof and the plan stated a bound it rests on, simulation only when it stated none; the hover text gives the simulation and LEC results and the stated bounds.

The plan, in full 5606 chars · not approved

I'll read the source around the candidate region and the surrounding context before deciding.

TIER: NONE

PLAN:

Decision. Keep revision 1 (3 blocks, A/A0 0.977, D/D0 0.997, product 0.974). I propose no further rewrite: the kept candidate already converts all nine candidate products, uses the provably minimum number of blocks for them, and its period is set by a path that the rewrite cannot touch. Every remaining idea I can justify from the source changes area by a few LUTs — far inside the ±4.6% seed noise — or makes the result worse.

Every candidate region, converted or left. All nine ($mul$raygentop.v:2874$348 … $mul$raygentop.v:2882$356) are already converted in the kept revision and none is left in soft logic: the six u/v products are the ax*ay/bx*by terms of blocks R, G, B, and the three w products are regrouped away by u·ru+v·rv+wl·rw ≡ u·(ru−rw)+v·(rv−rw)−rw (mod 128), lines 2862–2882 with wl = 255−u−v and the 7‑bit outputs at 2884–2886. There is no tenth multiply in bilinearintrp and nothing left to convert. Blocks added by this plan: 0 (the kept candidate's 3 stand).

Register stages, converted regions (unchanged from the kept revision, stated for completeness). All nine: before the rewrite 2 stages (stage 1 = ul/vl/wl and the nine weight registers, lines 2862–2873; stage 2 = the product registers i1r..i3b, lines 2874–2882; then a combinational 3‑input add into r/g/b). After: 2 stages (stage 1 = the block's operand registers, stage 2 = the block's result register, then the combinational 7‑bit subtract of rw2/gw2/bw2). Operands applied before edge k reach the output after edge k+1 in both.

Why 3 blocks cannot be reduced. After the mod‑128 regrouping the region is exactly six products, u·dcu and v·dcv for c ∈ {r,g,b}. One int_sop_2 provides two product slots, and chaining adds sums, not slots, so six products need three blocks unless two colours share one block by bit‑field packing. Packing is impossible here: putting colour g at bit offset K via ay = dru + (dgu<<K), by = drv + (dgv<<K) needs K+7 ≤ 19, i.e. K ≤ 12, while the low field u·dru + v·drv reaches 2·127·127 = 32258, so K ≥ 15 is required to keep its carry out of the high field. The operands cannot be shrunk further — u, v are already reducible only to 7 bits (mod 128) and the differences are 7 bits — and the carry floor(L/2^K) cannot be corrected from the block's output, since only L mod 128 is visible there. Three DSP tiles is therefore the floor, and it already equals what the baseline spent on these same nine multiplies (both rows measure 6 tiles), so no block-count move is available in either direction that helps.

Why the period cannot be improved by a rewrite. The measured seed‑1 path starts at $dffe~387^Q~4, crosses $add~377 and the 16‑stage $add~380 carry chain, then three LUT levels (new_n6116_1, new_n6115, n4829) and ends at a block operand register. That is the intersection‑unit arithmetic feeding u01a..u10c/v01a..v10c and the selectuv case mux of lines 2818–2858 — all of it upstream of, or inside, logic the rewrite must reproduce unchanged, and over 3.7 ns of it is wire. The same path exists in the baseline, ending at ul/vl instead (baseline 4.847 ns vs 4.834 ns here), which is why D/D0 is 0.997 and why the first pass's 6 blocks bought nothing on delay either. The block itself has ~2.6 ns of slack (its internal path is 2.219 ns), but nothing on the critical path is multiply‑add, so none of it can be moved inside. Moving the mux behind the block's operand register would add a third stage and break the cycle‑exact match; there is no other stage in bilinearintrp to give back (module input → stage 1 → products → combinational output).

Why the remaining soft-logic trims are not worth a measurement. What the rewrite still adds is six 7‑bit difference subtracts on the pad path, 42 flip-flops delaying rw/gw/bw by the same two stages, and three 7‑bit output subtracts. The 42 flip-flops are irreducible: the leftover term is forced to be −rw (it is the value of the sum at u=v=0, for every choice of expansion base), it must arrive two cycles late, and it cannot ride through the block, because a block can only emit a delayed copy of an operand that is multiplied by a power of two in one of its two slots, and both slots are committed to u·dru and v·drv. Narrowing ax/bx to u[6:0]/v[6:0] is legal (changing u by multiples of 128 leaves result[6:0] fixed) but deletes only the bit‑7 mux — a couple of LUTs on 4.65e6, two orders of magnitude below the seed noise. Replacing one colour's block with hand‑coded mod‑128 multipliers to save a DSP tile (253 779) costs two 7×7 truncated multipliers plus ~42 pipeline flip-flops, which I estimate at 280k–390k, i.e. a net loss, and it puts a multiply on the already-critical mux path.

The kept revision is therefore the best result available from this block on this region, and it is below the original (median product 2.24585e7 vs 2.30616e7).

DESIGN_CHANGE: The only remaining lever is the path the rewrite may not touch: if bilinearintrp could take one more cycle of latency on r,g,b, or if u01a..u10c/v01a..v10c (or the muxed u/v) arrived one register stage later from the intersection logic, the MIN/sub carry chain plus selectuv mux that now ends at the block's operand register would be split across two stages, cutting the register-to-register period well below 4.8 ns at the same 3 blocks and the same area.
